Greater China Technology Hardware  | Asia Pacific
Positive implications for
downstream tech hardware
companies from US
hyperscalers' 3Q26 results
We view US hyperscalers’ commentary and capex guidance as
positive for the Greater China supply chain. The next watch point
is the impact of hyperscalers' CY27 capex guidance on Greater
China tech hardware companies' C4Q26 earnings.

Greater China Technology Hardware
Asia Pacific
Industry View In-Line
Note: META, AMZN and GOOG are covered by

Brian Nowak. MSFT is covered by Adam Wood.
We view US hyperscaler commentary as positive for the Greater China supply
chain: The tone was uniformly positive, with demand still outstripping supply. With
CY26 nearly over, the focus was on CY27 capex. Although quantitative guidance was
not provided this quarter, US hyperscalers’ comments suggested that demand
would remain robust into CY27.
We stay OW on the Greater China supply chain: We like Wistron, a key L6 partner

to Nvidia and AMD, with new customers bringing more L10 (Lenovo and an AI lab)
and L11 AI server exposure (Amazon, Oracle). We also like Lenovo, a key general-
purpose server supplier to Microsoft and Oracle, but also a rising supplier of AI
servers, with ~US$54bn AI server pipeline as of C2Q26/F1Q27. We continue to like
Wiwynn, a key general-purpose server supplier to three hyperscalers (Microsoft,
Meta and Amazon) and a potential assembly partner for two new customers (a

hyperscaler and an AI lab). Wiwynn is also a key supplier of Trainium server racks, as
well as AMD Helios racks for Meta.  We like GCE and EMC/TUC as beneficiaries of
US hyperscalers’ demand for PCB and CCL in general-purpose and AI servers. We
like Unimicron and NYPCB, Greater China’s key ABF substrate suppliers for
Morgan Stanley does and seeks to do business with
companies covered in Morgan Stanley Research. As a result,
investors should be aware that the firm may have a conflict of

communications with a subject company, public appearances
and trading securities held by a research analyst account.
Key Takeaways
MSFT lowered its CY26 capex guidance by US$5bn to US$170bn, reflecting
delays in third-party capacity deliveries rather than a demand issue.
MSFT also guided for capex to “accelerate” in C1H27, driven mainly by CPUs
(higher memory & storage costs), GPUs and delayed third-party capacity
deliveries.
META narrowed its CY26 capex guidance to US$135-145bn (from US$130-145bn)

and said it would keep investing “aggressively” into CY27.
GOOG maintained its CY26 capex guidance of US$195-205bn and reiterated that
CY27 capex will increase “significantly” y/y.
AMZN maintained its CY26 capex guidance of US$220bn and said CY27 capex
would be “meaningfully” higher than CY26.
November 2, 2026 02:07 AM GMT

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

Document 172: 261105_daiwa_nypcb

Source PDF: all report pdf/261105_daiwa_nypcb.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-05

### Page 1

Summary: NYPCB released its 3Q26 results after market hours on 5 November. Net profit 
was TWD3.30bn (EPS: TWD5.10), 9% below our estimate and the Bloomberg consensus. 
Revenue rose 21% QoQ and 49% YoY to TWD16.38bn, in line with expectations. GM was 
29.3%, 1.0ppt below our estimate and 0.7ppt below consensus, reflecting yield losses 
during the Kunshan ramp-up and a more modest BT substrate margin uplift than we had 
assumed. OPM was 26.4%, versus our 27.5% estimate and 26.5% consensus. Despite 
operating profit being broadly in line, net profit was weighed down by a higher tax rate 
(26% vs. our 22% estimate), reflecting increased profitability at NYPCB’s China factories.

Looking ahead, we expect modest BT and c.10% ABF price hikes in 4Q26, which would 
result in mid-teens % QoQ growth in 4Q26E. This is consistent with its October revenue of 
TWD6.29bn (released 2 November, up 13% MoM and 70% YoY), equivalent to 34% of our 
4Q26 forecast (historic range being 33-39%). Per management, the Kunshan plant’s UTR 
has increased to 90-95% in October (vs. 80-85% in 3Q26 and 65-70% in 2Q26). As 
NYPCB is unwilling to accept LTAs with end clients (compared with its competitors), we 
believe its bargaining power should be the highest among TW substrate vendors. We have 
a Buy (1) rating on NYPCB, with a 12-month TP of TWD2,460, based on a target PER of 
49x applied to our 1-year forward EPS forecast. Key downside risk: weaker-than-expected 
demand for switches.

What’s the impact: 
Below is our first take on NYPCB’s 3Q26 results, released on 5 November.

• 3Q26 net profit missed expectations. Net profit came in at TWD3.30bn (EPS: 
TWD5.10), 9% below both our estimate (TWD3.63bn) and the Bloomberg consensus 
(TWD3.62bn). 3Q26 revenue of TWD16.38bn (up 21% QoQ and 49% YoY) was in-line 
with expectations, while the gross margin reached 29.3%, 1ppt/0.7ppt below 
our/Bloomberg consensus estimates due to loss on the yield rate as the Kunshan plant 
ramped up; our gross margin expansion assumption for BT substrate was also too 
bullish. Operating margin reached 26.4%, versus our 27.5% estimate and the street’s 
26.5%, mainly on the lower-than-expected gross margin. However, while the operating 
profit was largely on track with expectations, net profit was dragged down by a higher-
than-expected tax rate (26% vs. our 22% estimate) due to rising profitability from 
factories in China.

### Page 1

ab 5 November 2026
Global Research
First Read
Elan Microelectronics
Q426E likely to be the near-term trough
Q326 earnings below on softer NB demand environment
Elan's Q326 EPS of NT$0.58 (net profit NT$168mn) was below UBSe/street of NT
$1.29/1.71, as a result of lower revenue amid weaker NB demand environment. Q326
revenue was already reported at NT$2.2bn (-31% QoQ), below UBSe/street of NT
$2.4/2.8 bn as NB brand customers further reduced their orders from late-Aug on softer

end-demand after retail price hike and higher component inventories. GM was down
2.7ppts QoQ to 44.7% on less favourable mix, directionally in-line with UBSe of ~2ppt
QoQ decline. It booked non-op loss of NT$100mn, vs NT$294mn gain in Q226, mainly
due to unrealized loss on financial assets. Despite softer earnings, Elan remains
committed on its high dividend yield policy and is considering to raise its payout.
Near-term demand remains muted but Q426E likely to be the trough

Elan is restricted from providing Q4 financial guidance given the accumulated non-op
gain in past four quarters exceeded 10% of the net profit. With October sales reported
at NT$461 mn (-23% MoM), Elan expects Q426 remains a challenging quarter amid
softer demand environment and ongoing inventory adjustment. Nevertheless, we think
channel inventory is on track to be depleted in the coming months and Q426 is likely to
mark the trough for near-term revenue. We forecast Q426E sales to decline 38% QoQ in

Q426E but will rebound by 27% QoQ in Q127E on restocking. Into 2027, Elan noted it
has won some new projects at top NB brands, while also sees share gain opportunities
following its peer's business restructure, as well as ramping low-cost solution to capture
market trend. It also see good progress of haptics (2026 sales 5-6x vs 2025) and single
layer on-cell (3-4x), and we believe the momentum should continue into 2027.
ADAS and Drone remain key long-term growth drivers

Elan's AI-related revenue reached 14% of sales in Q326 and is expected to further
increase in Q426F as AI revenue remains more resilient vs NB-related products. Its ADAS
revenue is primarily driven by EV bus now and it thinks the proliferation into logistic
vehicles should be the next driver. Its drone-related business is on track to see higher
revenue contribution from 2027F as it has penetrated into Thunder Tiger's supply chain,
as well as two other customers, supplying both domestic and overseas demand. Elan

noted on top of the camera modules, its obstacle avoidance system, which carries much
higher ASP of US$800, could start to ramp from Q127F. We forecast its AI-business to
see a 45% sales CAGR in 2025-30E, outpacing traditional HMI.
Valuation: Maintain buy with a NT$150 price target
We cut 2026/27/28E EPS to NT$6.35/6.94/11.55 from NT$8.19/10.82/13.28 on softer
NB demand and prolonged inventory adjustment. We lower our PT to NT$150 (from NT
$170) using a lower multiple of 15x H227-H128E PE (from 16x 2027E P/E) to factor in

overall market valuation contraction. We view current headwinds as cyclical and believe
the market underappreciates Elan's AI opportunities and   upside from share gain.

Forecast returns

Company Description
Elan Microelectronics is a Taiwan-based fabless IC design house specialising in human-
machine interface including touchscreen controllers, touchpad modules, pointing stick
modules and fingerprint sensors, specifically for notebook PCs. It started with
microcontrollers (MCUs) for PC peripherals and consumer electronics upon establishment in

1994, and further expanded the reach into notebook PC input devices through the
acquisition of a related subsidiary under KOA Corporation in 2003.
Valuation Method and Risk Statement
Our price target is based on a target PE multiple.
Fabless integrated circuit (IC) design houses face a variety of uncertainties, including rapidly
changing technology, end-market demand, competition, employee turnover and foundry
wafer costs. They are also vulnerable to legal disputes over intellectual property rights.

For Elan, we believe key risks include: 1) PC and NB demand dynamics, with any slowdown in
these end-markets directly impacting Elan's growth and also its new product roll-outs; 2) a
longer-than-expected product qualification cycle or slower-than-expected product adoption
among clients; 3) fiercer-than-expected price competition from global and Asian peers
negatively impacting growth and gross margin; and 5) faster-than-expected foundry and
assembly cost increases negatively impacting gross margin.

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.

3Q26 Review: “조삼모사” Again  
3Q26P 연결 실적은 매출액 6조 943억원(YoY -6.0%), 영업이익 4,520억원(YoY -46.2%, OPM 
7.4%)을 기록하며 컨센서스 대비 연결 매출액(7.5조원)과 영업이익(1.16조원) 모두 대폭 하회했다. 주
요 자회사가 양호한 실적을 기록했음에도 불구하고, 지상방산 실적이 매우 부진했다. 지상방산은 매출
액 1조 2,455억원(YoY -41.0%), 영업이익 217억원(YoY -96.2%, OPM 1.7%)을 기록했는데, 1)3Q 
생산 물량이 4Q 매출 인식되는 흐름 속, 고정비 부담이 컸었고, 2)낮은 수출 비중(3Q 48%, 2Q 58%)
과 국내외 사업의 부진한 믹스(국내 BEP 수준, 해외 OPM 11% 추정), 3)비우호적인 원/달러 환율, 4)
대전 사업장 사고 복구 및 기타 일회성 비용(300억원+160억원, 총 460억원) 등이 반영되었다.   
대전 사업장 정상화 시점과 사업 차질 영향은? 
대전 사업장은 내년 상반기 중 정상화될 예정이며, 보은과 여수 공장에서 생산 보완을 위한 조치가 진
행 중인 것으로 소통했다. 또한 대전 사업장은 국내 물량 위주이며, 수출 납기(천무, 천궁-II 추진체 
등)에는 지장이 없을 것이라고 언급했지만, 기타 사업장 램프업을 위한 인건비 증가 등, 수익성 악화는 
불가피할 것으로 예상된다.  
15%대의 매출 성장 가이던스는 달성이 가능할까? 
보수적으로 접근하는 것이 합리적이라는 판단이다. 당사는 ‘26E 지상방산 매출액을 일부 ‘27~28E로 
이연하면서 -6.1% 하향 조정했다. 사측은 폴란드 EC1 천무 인도 가이던스를 기존 40문에서 60문으
로 상향 조정하는 등 납품 속도가 예정대비 빨라진 것을 언급했다. 다만, 천무 EC1에서 미사일 물량 
비중이 높은 것으로 파악되기 때문에 생산 지연 가능성과 수출입 데이터 상 12월 출하 물량의 매출 인
식 이월 가능성을 반영했다. ‘27E는 폴란드 후속 사업과 루마니아 K9, 호주 레드백 등이 양산 페이즈
에 진입하며 성장세가 지속될 전망이다.    
투자의견 Buy, 목표주가 1,480,000원 유지 
실적 쇼크와는 별개로, 미국 K9MH 시제, 스페인 K9, 크로아티아 천무 등 수주는 서프라이즈라고 판
단된다. 분기말 수주잔고는 35조원(수출 비중 74%, 스페인 미포함)으로 중장기 성장성에 대한 이견은 
없다. 불가피한 분기 실적 변동으로 동사의 미래 가치를 예단하는 것은 시기상조라는 판단이다.

### Page 1

(012450) 
한화에어로스페이스 
2026.10.30

AI로보틱스/방산/조선/운송 양승윤 
6139 / syyang0901@eugenefn.com

여전히 비중확대 의견, 그리고 Top-Pick 
 투자의견 BUY(유지) 
 목표주가 1,660,000원(유지) 
 현재주가 760,000원(10/29)

 3Q26 연결 매출액은 6조 943억원(-6.0%yoy), 영업이익은 4,520억원(-
46.2%yoy, OPM 7.4%)을 기록. 영업이익 컨센 1조 1,614억원을 하회.  
 지상방산의 손익이 크게 악화. 영업이익은 217억원(OPM 1.7%) 기록. 주요 
납품이 4분기에 집중된 가운데 일회성 비용 약 500억원(대전사업장 복구·
정상화 약 300억원, 국내 사업 충당금 약 160억원)과 환율 영향이 겹침.   
 다만 내용을 살펴보면 수출 사업의 수익성 훼손은 크지 않았다는 판단임.  
 일회성 제외 영업이익은 약 717억원(OPM 5.8%)이며, 일회성 비용은 대전
사업장이 국내 물량을 전담한다는 점에서 전액 국내 귀속으로 추정. 내수 
OPM을 일회성 제외 -5% 내외로 가정하면, 내수 영업이익은 약 -826억원
(일회성 포함 OPM -12.7%), 수출은 1,043억원(OPM 17.6%)으로 추정됨. 
 1Q26 대비로는 국내 마진 하락이, 2Q26 대비로는 수출 물량 감소가 가장 
크게 작용(도표 참고). 국내는 저마진 사업 매출이 늘고 고마진 반복 양산 물
량이 줄며 일시적으로 믹스가 악화. 수출은 폴란드 등 주요 인도가 정체되어 
매출이 감소한 가운데 저수익 사업 중심으로 믹스가 악화된 영향. 
 4Q26부터 회복 구간 진입 전망. 폴란드 K9 2차 실행계약 물량의 현지 인
도가 본격화되며, 회사는 2026년 폴란드 K9 30문 이상, 천무 발사대 60대 
이상(*연초 40대에서 상향) 인도를 계획. 수출입 데이터상으로도 폴란드·루
마니아향 출하가 대규모로 진행 중이며 폴란드향 부속류 수출도 정상적으로 
이어지는 것으로 확인. 일회성 소멸과 고마진 수출 집중으로 수출 OPM은 
30% 내외로 회복할 것. 다만 국내는 정상 가동(2027년 상반기 목표) 전까
지 한 자릿수 초반 마진에 머물 것으로 전망. 
 중장기 수익성 우려도 크지 않다는 판단. 2027~2029년 매출 연 15% 성
장 가이던스는 유지됐고, 내년 폴란드 K9·천무 인도 물량은 모두 올해 대비 
증가할 계획. 폴란드 사업 이후 높아진 수출 마진 레벨은 후속 사업에서도 
유지될 전망. 수주는 중동 지정학 불안으로 지연되고 있으나 대공체계 등 문
의가 이어지고 있어, 2027년부터 중동이 수주의 중심이 될 것으로 기대. 
 동사에 대한 투자의견 BUY, 목표주가 166만원을 유지함. 3Q26 지상방산 
쇼크는 국내 적자와 수출 물량 공백이 겹친 일시적 결과로, 수출 경쟁력 훼
손으로 보기 어려움. 실적 충격에 따른 주가 조정은 분할 매수 기회로 판단.  
 한화에어로스페이스의 주가는 4월 7일 사상 최고가 153.7만원에서 10월 
29일 76.0만원으로 -51% 조정 받은 상황. 12MF P/E 기준 14.3x, 27E 
P/E는 13.9x로 밸류에이션 관점에서도 매력적인 구간 진입. 그동안 3분기 
실적 우려 등으로 부진했다면, 이제는 실적 바텀아웃과 대형 수주 랠리 속 
다시 한 번 반등을 기록할 차례. 동사를 방산업종 Top-pick으로 유지함.

Compliance Notice 
당사는 자료 작성일 기준으로 지난 3 개월 간 해당종목에 대해서 유가증권 발행에 참여한 적이 없습니다 
당사는 본 자료 발간일을 기준으로 해당종목의 주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다  
당사는 동 자료를 기관투자가 또는 제 3 자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다 
조사분석담당자는 자료작성일 현재 동 종목과 관련하여 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다 
동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 조사분석담당자 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다 
동 자료는 당사의 제작물로서 모든 저작권은 당사에게 있습니다 
동 자료는 당사의 동의 없이 어떠한 경우에도 어떠한 형태로든 복제, 배포, 전송, 변형, 대여할 수 없습니다 
동 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료 및 정보로부터 얻어진 것이나, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 어떠
한 경우에도 자료는 고객의 주식투자의 결과에 대한 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙자료로 사용될 수 없습니다

Document 256: [329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 업종 내 여전히 매력적인 선택지HD현대중공업(329180) 업종 내 여전히 매력적인 선택지업종 내 여전히 매력적인 선택지 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 업종 내 여전히 매력적인 선택지HD현대중공업(329180) 업종 내 여전히 매력적인 선택지업종 내 여전히 매력적인 선택지 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-15
Pages: 5
Status: ok

### Page 1

HD현대중공업의 3Q26 연결 실적은 매출액 5조 8,602억원(yoy +33%, qoq -7%), 
영업이익 9,161억원(yoy +64%, qoq -12%, OPM 15.6%)으로 시장 컨센서스를 하회할 
전망이다. 동사의 3Q26 조업일수는 여름휴가, 추석연휴 및 중대재해 등 영향으로 
qoq -21% 감소했으며 환율 역시 qoq -84원 감소한 영향을 감안해 전분기 대비 
실적을 보수적으로 추정했다. 그럼에도 해양 부문 Trion FPU발 C/O에 따른 일회성 
환입이 예상됨에 따라 일부 이익 상쇄가 가능할 것으로 예상된다. 한편, 동사의 
임단협 부결로 당분기 추가적인 인건비 부담은 제한적일 것으로 판단되며 잠수함 
화재 사고 관련 비용 역시 4Q 이후 반영될 전망이다.

동사는 급격히 커져가는 AIDC향 전력수요 및 발전용 엔진 수주에 대응하고자 9/10 
총 4GW 규모의 4행정 중속엔진 캐파 증설을 위한 신규 투자를 공시헸다. 동사의 
기존 DC향 4행정 중속엔진 수주 단가를 고려 시 GW당 약 1조원의 매출 창출이 
가능할 전망이며 2행정 및 선박용 4행정 엔진 대비 높은 수익성이 예상되는 바 
생산이 본격화될 28년부터 동사 실적 성장성의 핵심 동인으로 작용할 전망이다.

동사에 대해 투자의견 BUY를 유지하며 업종 밸류에이션 디레이팅 및 시장 프리미엄 
조정에 따른 Target Multipe 변동으로 목표주가를 70만원으로 하향 조정한다. 상선 
시장 수주 경쟁 심화로 업종 밸류 조정이 이루어지고 있으나 동사는 엔진 기반 장기 
성장성으로 Peer 대비 상대적 매력도 확보 및 주가 방어가 가능하다는 판단이다.

Editorial 
Increasing signs of a bottom 
Strategy summary: Democrats’ thumping win in the US mid -
term elections has three key implications: i) policy gridlock is 
back in Washington, likely stalling fiscal expansion and new 
tariffs; ii) the chance of a prolonged Middle East conflict is 
curtailed, lowering oil prices and inflation expectations; and  iii) 
the pace of AI data-centre buildout is at risk , curbing AI-linked 
bond supply. Higher corporate and wealth taxes are a long-term 
risk if Democrats win the 2028 presidential elections.   
For portfolios, the election reinforce s our mid-cycle playbook 
already in place:  remaining broadly balanced across assets, 
while looking for tactical opportunities. As the S&P500 volatility 
index (VIX) surpasses 25, we see rising signs of equity market 
bottoming. We add an opportunistic idea on the US IT sector 
(see page 4), while using elevated volatility to earn income 
through structured strategies. In bonds, we prefer the 3-7-year 
part of the Developed Market bond yield curve and BBB-to-BB-
rated corporate bonds to earn attractive income as AI supply 
eases. In FX, we position for a modestly weaker USD over 6 -
12 months by allocating to ex-US assets.  
Policy implications:  Oil fell 12% over the past week as 
Democrats captured the US Senate and House. The party also 
flipped governorships in five states – Georgia, Iowa, Nevada, 
Ohio and Texas – securing a majority of governorships among 
50 US states for the first time since 2010. Vice President Vance 
and Republican leaders attributed the defeat to the Iran conflict, 
making it harder for President Trump to sustain it. Democrat 
control of the defence budget from next year further constrains 
conflict escalation. Despite near-term escalation risks, we see 
the geopolitical risk premium in oil prices fading, with oil easing 
towards USD 80/bbl  over 6-12 months. Oil-driven disinflation 
should allow central banks to adopt less aggressive policies. 
Adding tactical exposure to US IT sector : Semiconductors 
slumped this week amid concerns over data-centre moratoria, 
while beneficiaries of Democrat policies include defensives, 
affordability-related stocks, tariff rollback beneficiaries and  the 
healthcare sector. We use the volatility to add tactical exposure 
to the US IT sector . As we enter mid -cycle dynamics, equities 
should return 8-10% annually. The 2015–2017 mid-cycle offers 
a useful comparison: with an opposition Congress,  S&P 500 
returns slowed to 8.3% , but remained positive. Diversification 
across sectors and regions remains a priority amid the rotation. 
Prefer belly of the bond curve: Government bonds rallied on 
expectations of slower growth. We still expect two more Fed 
hikes by H1 2027, given sticky near-term inflation and a robust 
economy. However, cheaper oil should lower inflation towards 
the Fed’s 2% target next year, allowing rate cuts in H2 2027. 
Ultra-long yields remain elevated due to  fiscal challenges. 
Bonds, particularly those from non-hyperscaler investment -
grade corporates , offer attractive income. The 3 -7-year 
segment – the belly of the yield curve – offers the best balance 
of income and sensitivity to interest-rate changes. 
Credit to benefit from restrained AI supply:  Since states  
control data centre regulations, Democrat wins in governorship 
races in leading data centre states, such as Texas and Georgia, 
means growth in this segment is likely to be restricted. Slower 
data-centre construction could improve credit supply -demand 
conditions due to reduced issuance by hyperscalers. However, 
some data -centre-focused deals could become less viable, 
warranting caution towards lower -quality issuers. High -yield 
credit spreads have widened, but BB- and B-rated bonds have 
held up, while CCC -rated debt has r eached distressed levels. 
We prefer the BBB–BB range, spanning the boundary between 
investment-grade and high-yield debt, for income. 
A modest headwind for the USD, tailwind for gold: The 
dollar should remain range-bound near term before weakening 
modestly over 6-12 months. Democrat control of the Congress 
limits tariff escalation , fiscal expansion and rapid AI buildout , 
three pillars of US exceptionalism supporting the USD through 
2026. Disinflation should also encourage a less aggressive Fed 
stance, removing another source of USD support. Near -term 
support for the USD index (DXY) remains at 100.7 . A weaker-
than-expected payrolls report tonight could accelerate the 
USD’s decline, and vice versa . A softer dollar and sustained 
central-bank buying, led by China, remain supportive of gold.  
—  Rajat Bhattacharya

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 6 November 2026

Top client questions 
  What are the equity market implications of the US mid -
term election results?   
Our view: The US mid -term election outcome adds political 
headwinds but does not alter our outlook for positive equity 
returns. Investors should maintain a Core allocation to global 
equities and continue to diversify their equity exposure across 
regions and sectors. We initiate an opportunistic idea on the US 
information technology (IT) sector, moving our opportunistic 
idea positioning from a de fensive tilt to a more balanced 
positioning. Higher volatility also presents opportunities to use 
structured strategies for equity exposure.  
Rationale: The Democrats’ victory in both chambers of Congress 
has supported the outperformance of themes aligned with their 
policy priorities , with clean energy stocks , including solar 
companies, among the notable beneficiaries. Affordability-related 
themes have also benefited, including companies that could gain 
from potential tariff rollbacks. Medicaid-related stocks  have also 
risen on hopes that healthcare will gain from policy changes. 
Meanwhile, semiconductor stocks remain under pressure  amid 
concerns about potential moratoriums on data centres. Our AI capital 
expenditure (capex) estimates remain conservative relative to 
consensus, but with a potential reset in expectations around data 
centre spending, investors should remain selective.  
We initiate an opportunistic idea on the US IT sector after a 
significant 17% correction this quarter. Historically, this has been 
followed by strong positive returns over the next few quarters ( see 
table). Semiconductors, about 40% of US IT, appears oversold after 
correcting more than 30% since we closed our opportunistic idea a 
month ago. Earnings growth for US IT continues to be solid, with 
volume driven growth in semiconductors, AI monetisation 
opportunities, ongoing cloud growth and resilient services revenue.  
As we highlighted in our November 2026 Global Market Outlook , 
the transition to mid -cycle is typically characterised by greater 
political headwinds. The mid -term election outcome reinforces this 
trend. The mid-cycle experience of 2015-17 offers a useful historical 
parallel for returns that moderate, but remain positive. Although we 
trimmed global equities to a Core allocation last week, we continue 
to expect equities to deliver mid-to-high single-digit returns per 
annum during the mid -cycle, even amid political headwinds, 
supported by steady earnings growth in both 2027 and 2028. 
Stay invested and diversify, with Overweight allocations to the US 
and A xJ, which offer the strongest earnings growth. Sector 
diversification is key as we expect market leadership to broaden. We 
are Overweight the US IT, US healthcare, US financial, US materials, 
Europe ex-UK financial and Japan financial sectors. 
—  Fook Hien Yap, Senior Investment Strategist

Policy uncertainty and a change in the innovation 
cycle is consistent with our view of a mid-cycle 
transition, where equity returns moderate but 
remain positive

Standard Chartered Bank

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  What are the key takeaways from Q3 2026 US earnings? 
Our view:  Q3 US earnings remain robust, but more modest 
earnings beats reinforce our view that equity markets are 
entering a mid-cycle phase. We remain Overweight US equities, 
but recommend investors to maintain diversified sector 
exposure as returns moderate and market leadership broadens. 
Rationale: As of 30 October, 82% of S&P500 companies that ha d 
reported had beaten earnings estimates. However, aggregate 
earnings were just 4.5% above expectations, a smaller beat than in 
recent quarters. Still, Q3 US earnings growth remain s robust at 
31.6% y/y, supporting expected full-year growth of 36.1% in 2026 
and 16.4% in 2027 for the S&P500. The slower pace of upgrades 
suggests the bull market is maturing  as investors focus on the 
sustainability of earnings growth. This raises the hurdle for positive 
share price reactions and makes selectivity more essential. For Big 
Tech, capex commitments remain high, while free cash flow is under 
pressure and expected to remain negative. Financing risks weigh on 
sentiment, particularly for companies with weaker balance sheets.  
We remain Overweight US equities , given the strong earnings 
growth, but we expect elevated volatility  and modest returns. We 
prefer quality exposure within technology  while maintaining  
diversified exposure across US financials, healthcare and materials. 
—  Ryan Goh, Investment Strategist 
  What to expect from Japanese banks’ upcoming results?   
Our view:  We expect  Japanese banks’  earnings growth  to 
continue over the medium term, supported by higher domestic 
net interest income (NII) and resilient fee income. We remain 
positive on the sector, favouring banks with strong deposit 
franchises and scope to increase shareholder returns. 
Investors will focus on whether full -year guidance upgrades 
and buybacks exceed expectations. 
Rationale: The BoJ’s September rate hike to 1.25% took effect late 
in the quarter, so its benefit should be more visible from the 
December quarter as loans reprice. We expect three further BoJ 
rate hikes by June 2027, supporting further NII expansion.  We 
will also look for signs that stronger corporate investment is driving 
faster loan growth and higher financing and advisory fees.  
We prefer to place more weight on guidance upgrades driven by 
recurring lending and fee income than on those driven by gains on 
equity disposals. Continued reductions in cross -shareholdings 
could free up capital for additional buybacks, providing a further 
boost to return on equity  (ROE). Key risks:  faster-than-expected 
deposit rate repricing, rising credit costs and bond portfolio losses. 
—  Robin Xie, CFA, Senior Investment Strategist

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 6 November 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  What are the fundamental reasons behind your India 
equity upgrade? Which sectors offer favourable risk-reward? 
Our View: Indian equities offer an increasingly attractive risk -
reward profile. With India’s valuation premium to Emerging 
Markets (EMs) narrowing, foreign ownership near decade lows, 
Underweight EM positioning  and a stabilising Indian rupee 
(INR), conditions are increasingly supportive of Indian equity 
outperformance vs. the broader AxJ region.  
Rationale: We have upgraded Indian equities to Overweight within 
our AxJ equities allocation. While Indian markets  have witnessed 
USD 32bn of foreign investor outflows in 2026 year-to-date, strong 
domestic institutional investor inflows of USD 74bn  have more 
than offset foreign selling. These outflows and the Indian equity 
market’s underperformance vs . global peers reflect broader EM 
allocation shifts towards AI , rather than weakening India n 
fundamentals. Capital has gravitated toward s markets such as the 
US, Taiwan and  South Korea, which offer direct exposure to AI 
beneficiaries. However, we expect market leadership to broaden, as 
the rapid AI buildout is increasing ly debt-funded, and US mid-term 
election results raise uncertainty. We prefer to diversify and we see 
investors reallocating towards structurally strong  growth markets 
such as India, which offer non-AI driven growth.  
The RBI’s measures have supported INR stability,  but underlying 
USD demand remains elevated. Lower oil prices would improve 
external balances, but the INR is likely to follow a path of gradual, 
controlled depreciation rather than a quick downward move. On 
valuation to its own historical average, the Nifty 50 one-year forward 
P/E is trading 18% below its long-term average while ROE at 14.7% 
is above historical averages. With India now marginally Underweight 
in large EM portfolios compared to  the c.2% average Overweight 
historically, the case for a recovery in foreign flows is 
strengthening. 
India’s earnings growth remains intact . The Nifty 50 clocked Q1 
FY27 and Q2 FY27 profit-after-tax growth of c.20% y/y (ex -oil 
marketing companies) . Large -caps offer greater earnings visibility 
and downside protection, while stronger growth in small - and mid-
caps warrants a selective approach given elevated valuations.  
India’s growth outlook remains supported by a strengthening 
investment cycle, manufacturing momentum, supply chain 
diversification, resilient consumption and healthy corporate and 
banking balance sheets. Importantly, earnings growth remains 
robust and broa d-based across the financial, metals, telecom, 
automobiles and other domestic sectors, with Nifty 50 earnings 
growth expectations of c.14-15% over 2027 vis a vis A xJ’s 
technology driven earnings growth of 29% in 2027. 
—  Shaily Gang, Chief Investment Strategist, India

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  Does the emerging US job market data alter your view of 
the Fed’s rate path and government bond yields? 
Our view: We do not expect the recent and incoming US job 
market data to alter our outlook unless there are significant 
deviations from the US economy’s current trajectory.  
Rationale: The US economy continues to exhibit resilience, with the 
latest Job Openings and Labour Turnover Survey data showing job 
openings surprising to the upside at 7.5mn. Initial jobless claims 
remain near three-month lows. There is little evidence of a material 
slowdown in labour demand. Inflation is expected to trend lower 
through H2 2027, but the disinflationary impulse remains a medium-
term story and is unlikely to manifest meaningfully in the near term. 
The market is now pricing in two additional 25bps hikes by H1 
2027 (down from three previously), in line with our view. These two 
rate hikes are largely priced into the US government bond yield 
curve. We estimate 2- and 10-year US government bond yields to 
range between 4.5-4.75% and 5-5.25%, respectively, over the next 
three months . Our twelve -month forecast for 2- and 10 -year US 
government bond yields are 4.25-4.5% and 4.75-5%, respectively, 
The 2-year yield is already trading within our expected range and 
would only move materially higher if a significantly stronger -than-
expected payrolls print led markets to price in a third hike. With two 
hikes baked in, long -run disinflation expectations intact and an 
uncertain fiscal outlook, the longer end of the  yield curve is 
expected to be less sensitive to a single payrolls release. 
—  Ray Heung, Senior Investment Strategist 
  What is your outlook for the GBP following the BoE’s rate 
hike this week? Do you see any impact on EUR/GBP? 
Our view: The BoE  rate hike supports  the GBP, but upside 
remains limited. We remain constructive on EUR/GBP, 
expecting a rebound towards 0.8450. 
Rationale: The BoE hiked by 25bps to 4% as expected . The GBP 
was little changed, despite hawkish inflation guidance. With markets 
pricing only around 50bps of additional BoE tightening over the next 
12 months, we see the GBP remaining vulnerable. In contrast, the 
ECB held its deposit rate at 2.50% in October but signalled readiness 
to tighten if inflation remains elevated. We expect Euro area inflation 
to rise further, keeping the ECB’s tightening bias alive. This suggests 
EUR/GBP has fallen too far relative to interest rate differentials.  
Germany’s planned increase in federal debt issuance  should 
support Euro area growth, while Moody’s decision not to downgrade 
France has removed an important EUR tail risk. Meanwhile, tighter 
UK fiscal policy is likely to weigh on domestic growth and business 
investment. We expect EUR/GBP to recover towards 0.8450. 
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist 
—  Iris Yuen, Investment Strategist

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  Why is the AUD underperforming despite the RBA hike? 
Our view: The AUD has lagged because markets had largely 
priced in the RBA’s November rate hike to 4.85%, and the bank 
gave investors less reason to expect another. Equity weakness 
also weighed on the currency. We remain bullish on AUD/USD  
and would add on pullbacks. Australia’s interest rate advantage 
and a sustained recovery in risk sentiment should support a 
move towards 0.72 in three months and 0.74 in 12 months. 
Rationale: Ahead of the 3 November RBA rate decision, market 
pricing implied an 88% chance of a 25bp s hike, leaving little scope 
for an upside surprise. What changed after the announcement 
was the pricing of the next hike.  The RBA dropped its reference 
to further rate increases, and three members preferred a rate hold. 
Markets scaled back the odds of rates reaching 5.10% next year to 
around 30%, thus weighing on the AUD. 
The AUD is sensitive to swings in global risk appetite, especially 
the US tech sell-off spread to global equities, triggering margin calls 
and weighing on  the currency. An equity recovery would ease that 
pressure. Even after the two 25bp s Fed hikes we expect by mid -
2027, Australia would retain a 35bps rate advantage  over the top 
of the Fed’s target range, supporting a gradual AUD recovery. 
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist 
—  Iris Yuen, Investment Strategist

recovery in risk sentiment

Document 355: India_ A middle class too small to supplant China’s economic role (2026-10-26)

Source PDF: all report pdf/India_ A middle class too small to supplant China’s economic role (2026-10-26).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-26
Pages: 8
Status: ok

### Page 1

The bank
for a changing
world
At a time when Chinese consumption is slowing significantly, could India become a driver of global consumption? For now, this
prospect presents considerable difficulties, with many obstacles to overcome. Despite dynamic growth and the Indian economy’s
expanding share of global output (8.2% of worldwide GDP in 2025), the middle-class consumer population remains modest and is nowhere
near the size of China’s.
This is due to the concentration of employment in low-value-added and largely informal sectors. The few manufacturing sectors with

high value-added and strong job potential — electrical equipment, electronics, automotive, chemicals and pharmaceuticals — continue to
face constraints from an unfavourable business environment and a skills shortage that is holding back their move into premium market
segments.
Furthermore, the highest-paying service sector jobs, particularly in information technology, are now threatened by the rise of
artificial intelligence. Without concrete measures, the “demographic window” — which is expected to remain favourable for another 15 to

25 years — could close before India establishes a solid industrial base and a sufficiently large middle class.
INDIA: A MIDDLE CLASS TOO SMALL TO SUPPLANT CHINA’S ECONOMIC ROLE
Johanna Melka
issue 20
26 October 2026

### Page 2

2
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoInsight 20 / 26 October 2026
The bank
for a changing
worldINDIA: A MIDDLE CLASS TOO SMALL TO SUPPLANT CHINA’S ECONOMIC ROLE
INDIA IS STRUGGLING TO ESTABLISH A ROBUST MIDDLE
CLASS
With an annual growth rate of 7.3% over the past decade (excluding the
pandemic), India has one of the highest growth rates among the world’s
major economies. Yet, its middle-class consumer base remains surpri-
singly small. When measured in current U.S. dollars, total consumption

is very high in India (USD 2,241 billion in 2025), but this figure prima -
rily reflects the country’s overall population size. By comparison, this
consumption level falls 3.5 times short of China’s corresponding figure.
Furthermore, per capita consumption adjusted for purchasing power
parity (PPP) is 1.3 times lower than that of Indonesia, the Philippines
and Vietnam. When evaluated at constant prices, consumption per ca-
pita has increased only slightly over the past thirty years compared to

China and even Vietnam (Chart 1).
According to our estimates, based on the research conducted by Ló -
pez-Calva and Ortiz-Juarez (2014) 1, India’s middle-class consumer
segment accounts for just 1.6% of the total population (see Table 1),
or fewer than 24 million people. This is less than in Thailand and Viet-
nam (more than 30 million each) and far below China (approximately
490 million, according to the same definition).
The majority of households identified as middle class by standard In -

dian research institutions (the Centre for Monitoring Indian Economy
[CMIE] and the National Council of Applied Economic Research [NCAER])
would, in fact, be categorised as “vulnerable” according to World Bank
criteria: households that have officially emerged from poverty yet lack
the financial stability necessary for sustained economic security.
Highly concentrated consumption and limited
upward mobility
The structure of Indian household consumption reveals significant

disparities between urban and rural areas. In 2024, average month -
ly consumption per capita amounted to 6,996 rupees in urban areas
1 These authors define the middle class as individuals for whom the risk of falling back into poverty is limited (less than 4% over four years), corresponding to daily per capita income
ranging from 17 to 98 2021 PPP dollars (with vulnerability thresholds at 6.85 and 14 2017 PPP dollars, according to the World Bank). In the absence of reliable national income

data collection mechanisms in India, our study applies these thresholds to the World Bank’s consumer expenditure data, an approach aligned with the objective of assessing actual
purchasing capacity deployed in the market (although this tends to slightly underestimate the middle class in terms of income, as household savings remain unaccounted for in
expenditure measurements).
(approximately USD 84.5), compared with 4,122 rupees in rural areas
(less than USD 50). The wealthiest 5% of urban consumers spent an

average equivalent of USD 245 per month per person. Consequently,
discretionary spending power remained concentrated among the hi -
ghest income brackets.
The structure of consumption has changed over the past decade. The
proportion allocated to food purchases (34.8% of total spending on ave-
rage) declined by 4.3 percentage points between 2011–12 and 2023–
24, while allocations to transportation and communications increased
to 12.4% (+3.8 pp). Although the allocation to durable goods has in -

creased, it remains below 7% of total household spending. This increase
was driven more by a rise in the use of credit than by income growth.
Over the past ten years, consumer loans have been the main source of
household debt. Although still modest relative to GDP , household debt
rose by 12.2 pp to reach 47.8% of GDP by the end of 2025, according to
the Bank for International Settlements (BIS).

The bank

world
THE LABOUR MARKET SUFFERS FROM STRUCTURAL
WEAKNESSES
The limited size of the middle class and the low level of household
consumption in India can be attributed, in particular, to the enduring
structural weaknesses of the labour market. The labour market faces
two major problems: insufficient job creation and the fact that most of
the jobs created are low-skilled and low-paid.

Insufficient job creation
Despite the Indian economy’s robust growth, its employment rate re -
mains substantially lower than in comparable Asian economies: 52.7%
in 2025 according to the International Labour Organization (ILO), com-
pared with 65.6% in Indonesia and 71.3% in Vietnam. This disparity
is mainly due to low labour force participation among women (34%
compared with 53.4% and 68%) and young people (40.8% of those aged
20-24 compared with 66.7% and 69.4%).

Over the past ten years, employment growth has averaged just 2.4%
annually, whereas GDP growth was close to 6% per year.
The services sector (representing 33.2% of employment in 2025) ac -
counted for the bulk of job creation during this period (+3.2% per year),
far ahead of the manufacturing sector (+1.4%) and, above all, agricultu-
re (+0.1%), which nevertheless remains the country’s largest employer
(41.5% of the workforce). This is not enough to absorb the 10 to 12 mil-

lion new entrants to the labour market each year.
2 Employment elasticity remained relatively stable between 1990 and 2012.
A limited number of sectors have significant po-
tential for creating high-quality, well-paid jobs
To identify sectors with high potential for job creation, we calculate
employment elasticity both for aggregate economy (KLEMS data) and
for the formal manufacturing sector alone (Annual Survey of Industries,
2025).
Over the period 1981–2023, aggregate employment elasticity remained

low at a virtually unchanged level, close to 0.3. This stability, however,
masks significant sectoral divergences: elasticity remained stable in
the services sector throughout the period (0.5), but it declined shar -
ply in the manufacturing sector (from 0.4 during 1981–1990 to just
0.1 during 2012–2023 2): at the same growth rate, India now creates
far fewer industrial jobs per unit of growth than it did forty years ago
(Table 2). In the formal manufacturing sector alone, employment elas-

ticity reached 0.7 over the past ten years, driven by very robust job
creation. However, this positive trend has not been sufficient to offset
the decline in informal sector employment, which accounts for 73.4%
of total manufacturing employment but only 38% of the sector’s value
added. Labour productivity in the formal manufacturing sector is thus
estimated to be 5.4 times higher than in the informal sector.
In capital-intensive sectors, employment elasticity ranged from 0.5 to

0.6 (including electrical equipment, transportation, chemicals and me-
tals), whereas in labour-intensive sectors — specifically wood, textiles
and apparel, and manufacturing food — employment elasticities re -
mained particularly low, or in certain cases, negative.

4
EcoInsight 20 / 26 October 2026 economic-research.bnpparibas.com
The bank
for a changing
world
In other words, growth in value added in these sectors with high labour 
absorption potential is primarily driven by productivity gains rather 
than job creation. Furthermore, even among companies operating in 
the formal sector, employment elasticity remains low at an average of 
0.4. Consequently, the traditional mechanism facilitating labour mobi-
lity from the agricultural sector to the labour-intensive manufacturing 
sector has been very limited over the past forty years.
The only manufacturing subsectors that combine strong growth, high 
employment elasticity, and high productivity — thereby offering real 
potential for creating high-quality jobs — are electronics and infor -
mation technology, electrical equipment, the automotive industry, the 
chemical industry, and, to a lesser extent, the pharmaceutical industry. 
In these subsectors, the formal employment elasticity ranges from 0.8 
to 1.2 (Chart 2). However, in 2025, these jobs accounted for just 1.4% 
of total employment and 5.9% of GDP . The potential therefore remains 
negligible at the macroeconomic level.
Several structural constraints limit job creation in 
India
- The high rigidity of the labour market: it consists mainly of small, in-
formal businesses — those with fewer than 50 employees account for 
65% of the productive sector — largely due to the stringency of labour 
legislation (for example, companies with more than 300 employees 
cannot carry out mass layoffs without authorisation).
- Numerous obstacles impede the growth of industrial and job-crea -
ting businesses: an unfavourable business environment (particularly 
difficulties in accessing land acquisition to build factories); high costs 
of accessing capital (particularly for small and medium-sized enter -
prises); insufficient export competitiveness in labour-intensive sectors 
(textiles, clothing), which encounter disadvantages, including less ad -
vantageous tariff conditions3 than competing Asian economies.
- The mismatch between the skills of the available workforce and the 
needs of businesses. This mismatch constrains wage growth and the 
development of household purchasing power, obstructs industrial ad -
vancement, and, consequently, the creation of high-quality jobs.
INADEQUATE JOB QUALITY DUE TO A LACK OF  
APPROPRIATE SKILLS
A predominantly informal and low-paid labour  
market
According to the ILO, 87.2% of total employment in India is informal, 
with 73.4% concentrated in the manufacturing sector alone, predomi -
nantly engaged in low-value-added activities that require few skills. 
Furthermore, the Periodic Labour Force Survey (PLFS) 2023/24 indi -
cates that 58.4% of Indian workers are self-employed, 19.8% are day 
labourers, and only 21.7% are regular employees — the highest-paying 
status, with a wage gap of more than 60% compared to the other two 
categories. According to the ILO, 20% of the population captures 79% of 
aggregate labour income. The agricultural sector, which still employs 
41.5% of the labour force while contributing less than 15% to GDP , is

3 Historically, tariff rates imposed on Indian products exported to Europe have been higher than those applied to certain other Asian countries (Bangladesh, Vietnam, Pakistan).
4 According to the World Bank and the NCAER, educational and training programmes are too theoretical and fail to develop cognitive and interpersonal skills. Vocational education 
and training are not designed to meet the needs of high-demand sectors. Collaborative relationships between educational institutions and employers — particularly regarding 
internships and on-the-job training — remain inadequately developed.
3.7 times less productive than the services sector, with a median hourly 
wage that is 40 to 50% lower.
A widespread skills shortage
This concentration of earnings reflects the low skill levels of the jobs 
held: according to the PLFS, 88.2% of workers hold jobs requiring little 
or no skill. The proportion of skilled jobs is just 11.2% and has declined 
by 4.6 pp since 2018. The underlying challenge is not a shortage of 
graduates but rather a misalignment between what the labour market 
needs and the educational preparation provided to graduates 4, which 
results in their gradual downward mobility into positions far below 
their formal qualifications.
Unemployment, the downward mobility of graduates, and a shortage 
of technical skills
This disparity between skills and business needs directly contributes to 
unemployment among graduates, which reached 13.5% in 2025 accor-
ding to the ILO, compared with 1.3% for individuals without a degree. 
Within the 25- to 34-year-old demographic, the unemployment rate 
stands at 15.2% for graduates (19.2% for those with a master’s de -
gree), compared with an average of 6.1% across this age group. Only 
8.3% of graduates secure positions that genuinely correspond to their 
educational attainment; 53% of bachelor’s degree holders and 36% of 
master’s degree holders are overqualified for their positions.
In low-skilled jobs, the lack of technical training represents a signifi -
cant barrier to youth employment: according to the World Bank’s En -
terprise Survey (2022), 17.8% of traditional service companies and 14% 
of “intermediate” manufacturing companies cite this as a major obsta-
cle. In India’s manufacturing sector, 32.9% of positions are reportedly 
held by underqualified workers, according to the ILO, compared with 
28.8% in Indonesia and just 16.5% in Vietnam.
A direct impediment to industrial advancement
A survey conducted by the Ministry of Skill Development & Entrepre -
neurship across seven key sectors — automotive, textiles, agriculture, 
livestock, IT, commerce and green energy — reveals that the pace of

5
EcoInsight 20 / 26 October 2026 economic-research.bnpparibas.com
The bank
for a changing
world
workforce training development significantly lags behind industry re -
quirements. This forces companies to train a considerable number of
young graduates themselves before they are ready for the workforce.
According to the Telecom Talent in the 5G Era report by the Telecom
Sector Skill Council, only 40% of IT and telecommunications graduates
are actually immediately employable.

This disparity in skills particularly constrains the transition towards
higher-value-added manufacturing activities within the sectors iden -
tified above (automotive, electronics and chemicals). Their growth
trajectory depends on technological advancement — electric vehicles,
semiconductors, specialty chemicals — and therefore on intermediate
to advanced technical skills that are currently lacking. Should trai -
ning provision fail to align with industry demand, employment elasti -

city and growth in these sectors will stagnate or decline, undermining
the only source of high-quality job creation currently available — and
consequently, the Indian economy’s ability to expand its middle class.
These structural limitations affecting national industrial development
diminish its appeal to foreign investors, despite its size. The stock of
foreign investment in India stood at USD 560 billion in Q2 2026, repre-
senting just 15% of GDP (compared with 20% in China, more than 52%

in Vietnam, and around 67% of GDP in Thailand and Malaysia).
ARTIFICIAL INTELLIGENCE: A NEW RISK TO JOB CREATION
Artificial intelligence (AI) may also limit the expansion of high-paid
employment opportunities in the service sector. Its deployment poses
a significant threat to employment in India, particularly in the hi -
ghest-paying service sectors — the same sectors that possessed si -
gnificant potential for expanding India’s middle-class consumer base.

Agriculture (41.5% of the labour force), construction (12%), and trade/
transportation (18%) remain largely immune to the direct replacement
of workers by AI, which reduces the overall macroeconomic scale of
the coming shock. The service sectors offering the most lucrative em -
ployment — IT, finance and insurance — account for just 1.3% and 1.2%
of total employment, yet these remain the primary targets of automa-
tion. In IT services companies, the adoption of AI is expected to reduce

the need for entry-level roles (quality assurance, software testing and
support roles) but increase demand for positions centred on platform
development and management oversight. Current sector-specific evi -
dence indicates a notable slowdown in job creation within IT services
but no widespread contraction. A study by the Indian Council for Re -
search on International Economic Relations (ICRIER) on the IT sector
confirms a decline in job creation for routine and entry-level roles.

While new professions are emerging such as data centre operators, AI
DevOps engineers who serve as intermediaries between software deve-
lopment and systems operations teams, and ethical AI specialists, the
volume of employment opportunities generated remains substantially
lower than the number of jobs at risk. Furthermore, the skill require -
ments for these newly created positions exceed those possessed by the
existing cohort of graduates.
REFORMS ARE A STEP IN THE RIGHT DIRECTION BUT DO

NOT GO FAR ENOUGH
Since 2019, the Modi government has implemented a series of mea -
sures to develop industry, attract foreign direct investment, and gain
greater integration in global trade (after decades of protectionist po -
licies).
Corporate subsidy programmes (Production-Linked Incentives) have
helped support the development of those sectors with the greatest
potential for job creation (electrical, electronics, chemicals, and auto -
motive). Similarly, labour market reform (which came into force in late

2025) may alleviate impediments to business expansion and the crea-
tion of formal jobs. However, its actual impact will depend on its adop-
tion by the states, which retain considerable discretionary autonomy
over labour law, and on its effective implementation on the ground.
The free trade agreement signed between India and the European
Union in late January 2026 (which is expected to take effect in 2027 at
the earliest) should mitigate the tariff disadvantage experienced by la-

bour-intensive industries: the EU has pledged to remove tariffs, which
currently range from 4% to 12% on textiles and clothing.
However, these measures alone will be inadequate for creating
high-quality jobs if the education system fails to meet the needs of
Indian businesses. It is therefore imperative that India continues to
improve its business environment and, even more importantly, reform
its education system to promote job creation in high-value-added and

well-paying sectors. Without concrete measures, the “demographic
window” — which is expected to remain favourable for another 15 to
25 years — could close before India establishes a robust industrial
foundation and a substantial middle class.

*****
While India’s economic growth has demonstrated considerable dy -
namism, this growth has not translated into the development of the
middle class. The real medium-term concern involves the emergence
of a “neo-precarious” middle class — substantial in terms of num -
bers but lacking the foundations necessary for advancing towards a
high-income economy (households with stable employment, the ability

to save, and social protection). The creation of formal, high-quality jobs
is currently inadequate to sustain industrial expansion and its progres-
sion towards higher-value segments. The cultivation of certain niche
activities, while promising, remains very limited and is far from benefi-
ting the entire population. Finally, the development of AI poses more of
a risk than a genuine opportunity for the country’s development. India
lacks both the infrastructure and the educational system necessary to

deploy AI in a way that would yield tangible benefits for its economy
and labour market.
Article completed on 19 October 2026

Document 382: NZドル相場の行方を左右する総選挙とRBNZの政策運営 ～インフレ圧力は根強い一方、政権交代に伴う政策枠組みの変更が不透明要因に～

Asia Trends

2026 年10 月 22 日

1/3 
NZ ドル相場の行方を左右する総選挙と RBNZ の政策運営 
～インフレ圧力は根強い一方、政権交代に伴う政策枠組みの変更が不透明要因に～ 
 経済調査部 主席エコノミスト  西濵 徹 
（℡：050-5474-7495）

（要旨） 
○ ニュージーランドでは、インフレ高進を背景に RBNZ が９月に２会合連続の利上げを実施し
たものの、11 月７日の総選挙を巡る不透明感がＮＺドル相場の重しとなってきた。 新興政党
のオポチュニティ党が連立協議のカギを握る可能性が高まっているうえ、仮に左派・労働党
を中心とする政権への交代が実現すれば、RBNZ の政策目標に雇用の最大化が再び加えられる
と見込まれる。そのため、追加利上げのハードルが高まる可能性が意識されている。 
○ ７-９月のインフレ率は前年比＋3.7％と鈍化し、市場予想も下回ったものの、引き続き目標
レンジを上回っている。エネルギーや食料品を中心にインフレ圧力が後退する一方、サービ
ス物価には上昇圧力が広がる兆しがうかがえる。さらに、原油価格の高止まりや干ばつによ
る食料インフレの懸念も残る。金融市場では早期利上げ観測がくすぶるものの、今回の物価
統計を受けて見方が修正される可能性もあり、先行きのＮＺドル相場は総選挙の結果と、そ
の後のRBNZ の政策枠組みに関する議論に左右される展開が予想される。

【RBNZは追加利上げ実施も、総選挙を巡る不透明感がNZドル相場の重しとなる展開が続いた】 
ニュージーランドでは、RBNZ（ニュージーランド準備銀行）が９月の定例理事会において２会合
連続の利上げを決定した （注１） 。 背景には、2025 年以降のインフレ率が加速してRBNZ の定める目
標レンジ（１～３％）の上限を上回る伸びで推移していることがある。さらに、イラン情勢の悪化
による原油価格の上昇がエネルギーインフレを招き、４-６月のインフレ率は前年比＋4.1％と2023
年 10-12 月以来の高い伸びとなった。こうした事情は、RBNZ の断続的な利上げ実施を後押ししたと
考えられる。 
一方、通貨ＮＺドル相場は上値の重い展開をみせてきた。同国では、11 月７日に議会（代議院：
一院制 （総議席数120） ） の総選挙の実施が予定されており、選挙結果を巡る不透明感が影響してい
るとみられる。世論調査によれば、右派の国民党、ないし左派の労働党を中心とする連立政権がい
ずれも多数派を形成できず、新興政党・オポチュニティ党がキャスティングボートを握る可能性が

注１ ９月２日付レポート「RBNZ は緩やかな引き締め示唆､NZ ドル相場の行方はどうなる？」 
  （https://www.dlri.co.jp/report/macro/654752.html）

### Page 2

2/3 
浮上している（注２） 。 同党は、都市部で年間1.75％、 農村部で0.5％の土地税を新設し、 それを財
源の一部に原則18 歳以上の国民に年間1.94 万ＮＺドルのベーシックインカム （ＢＩ） に相当する
「市民所得」を支給する税制改正を公約に掲げる。国民党、労働党ともに同党が掲げる税制改正案
の採用を否定しており、実現のハードルは高い。しかし、それ以外の政策の方向性については、左
派陣営と比較的親和性が高いとみられるため、選挙後に行われる連立協議の行方が注目される。 
こうしたなか、政治的な争点のひとつにRBNZ（ニュージーランド準備銀行）の政策運営が浮上し
ていることも注目される。労働党を中心
とする左派陣営 は、RBNZ のマンデート
（政策目標）の変更を公約に掲げてお
り、仮に政権交代が実現すれば、持続可
能な雇用の最大化が再び加えられると
見込まれる。足元の雇用環境は、直近４
-６月の失業率が10 年ぶりの高水準とな
ったことが確認されている（図１）。この
ため、RBNZ のブレマン総裁は９月会合後
の記者会見において、選挙結果は政策決
定に影響しないとの見方を示したもの
の、追加利上げのハードルが高まることは避けられない。こうした事情も、ＮＺドル相場の重しに
なってきたと考えられる。

【金融市場はRBNZの早期利上げを意識するも、NZドル相場の行方は総選挙の結果がカギを握る】 
このため、金融市場においては、RBNZ
の政策運営を占ううえで 物価動向に注
目が集まった。７-９月の消費者物価は
前年比＋ 3.7％に伸びが鈍化したもの
の、 ５四半期連続で目標レンジを上回る
推移となった（図２）。ただし、 この水準
は RBNZ が９月会合に際して示した見通
し（前年比＋3.9％）を下回るとともに、
事前の市場予想をも下回る伸びにとど
まった。前期比も＋0.66％と前期 （同＋
1.49％）から上昇ペースが鈍化してお
り、 原油価格の上昇一服を受けてガソリンなどエネルギー価格が下落したほか、 生鮮品をはじめと

### Page 3

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
3/3 
する食料品価格の上昇ペースが鈍化しており、 生活必需品を中心にインフレ圧力は後退した。一方
で、食料品やエネルギーを除いたコアインフレ率は前年比＋2.9％と前期（同＋2.6％）から伸びが
加速したものの、 引き続き目標レンジ内で推移している。 とはいえ、 前期比は＋1.1％と前期 （同＋
0.4％） から上昇ペースが加速しており、 財価格は比較的落ち着いた動きをみせる一方、 サービス物
価で幅広く上昇圧力が強まる動きが確認されるなど、 インフレ圧力が徐々に広がりをみせる兆しも
うかがえる。 
足元では、 イラン情勢を巡る不透明感を理由に原油価格は高止まりしており、 先行きもエネルギ
ーインフレが長引く可能性が高まっている。さらに、エルニーニョ現象の発生を受けて、同国では
東部や北部を中心に、乾燥・干ばつによる農業生産への悪影響が懸念されており、食料インフレや
景気の下押しが懸念される状況にある。 
総選挙においてはインフレも主要な争
点となっており、早期のインフレ抑制に
向けて、RBNZ が前倒しで利上げに動くと
の見方も金融市場に出ており、このとこ
ろの ＮＺドル相場が持ち直 す一因とな
ってきた可能性がある （図３） 。今回の物
価統計が市場予想を下回ったこと を受
けて、 早期利上げ観測が修正される可能
性もある。先行きのＮＺドル相場の行方
を巡っては、総選挙の結果と、その後の
RBNZ の政策枠組みに関する議論に左右
される可能性にも注意が必要と考えられる。

CENTRAL BANK MEETINGS

Bank of Thailand (BOT) (October 28)

We expect the BOT to maintain its policy 
rate at 1.00% at its 28 October meeting, 
extending its pause for a fourth 
consecutive decision as it balances 
inflationary pressures against uneven 
and subdued economic growth. Our view 
is aligned with fixed market income 
pricing. Headline inflation rose anew to 
2.8% yoy in September 2026, driven by 
higher energy, food, and core price 
increases, but remained within the upper 
end of the central bank’s 1 -3% target 
range. As the BOT is still likely to look 
through what it views as temporary, 
supply-driven inflationary pressu res, it 
will likely refrain from tightening policy, 
and keep interest rates accommodative 
to support the soft and uneven growth 
cycle.

European Central Bank (October 29)

We expect the ECB to extend its pause, 
keeping the deposit facility rate at 2.5%. 
Market participants will instead focus on 
the central bank’s assessment of rising 
French and Italian bond yields and 
widening spreads versus Bunds. While 
we do not expect dir ect references to 
individual countries, the ECB is likely to 
signal that recent developments in 
France reflect the country’s weaker 
fiscal and debt profile rather than a 
broader systemic concern. Policymakers 
will seek to differentiate between a 
localised bond sell -off and a Eurozone -
wide market disruption, justifying their 
decision to monitor developments rather 
than implement specific measures at this 
juncture. Meanwhile, inflation continues 
to rise, with little relief expected in 
upcoming readings given elevated 
global fuel prices. 
Bank of Japan (October 30)

The Bank of Japan is expected to keep 
the overnight call rate unchanged at 1.25% 
at this meeting, pausing after the 25bp 
hike in September. The BOJ is likely to 
assess the impact of the previous rate 
hike before taking further action. The 
recent stabilisat ion of the yen also 
reduces the urgency of another hike at 
this meeting.

The tightening bias should remain intact. 
Growth and inflation remain broadly on 
track, leaving room for the BOJ to revise 
up its FY2026 –FY2027 GDP and CPI 
forecasts. Wage growth has also 
remained strong, with Rengo reportedly 
targeting wage increases of 6% in 2027, 
reinforcing the BOJ’s confidence that 
underlying inflation can be sustained 
around its 2% target. We continue to 
expect the policy rate to rise to 1.50% in 
December 2026 or January 2027, 
followed by a further increase to 1.75% 
by 2Q 2027.

FORTHCOMING DATA 
RELEASES

Hong Kong SAR

Export growth is expected to accelerate 
from 53.0% yoy in August to 55.0% in 
September, in line with the pickup in 
China’s export growth from 25.0% to 
36.3%. As a key re-export hub for China,

### Page 3

The Week Ahead October 23, 2026 
                                                                                                                                         3

Hong Kong should benefit from robust 
AI-related electronics trade, with 
electrical machinery, equipment and 
parts exports to Hong Kong accounting 
for 20.6% of China’s total exports. 
Import growth is also expected to remain 
firm, supported by resilient expo rt 
orders and gradually improving 
investment sentiment, sustaining 
demand for intermediate and capital 
goods.

Singapore

Singapore’s industrial production (IP) 
likely remained in expansion for a 13th 
consecutive month in September 2026, 
although we expect growth to moderate 
to 7.6% yoy from August’s strong 15.4% 
yoy pace. IP growth continued to be 
underpinned by the electronics and 
precision engineering clusters, which 
has been key drivers of Singapore’s 
manufacturing strength, reflecting 
sustained demand for artificial 
intelligence-related hardware. However, 
performance across clusters remained 
uneven, with chemicals and biomedical 
manufacturing likely contracting, and 
tempering overall gains.

South Korea

The preliminary estimate for 3Q26 GDP 
is likely to show growth remaining solid 
at 3.2% yoy , slightly below 3.7% in 2Q 
and 3.8% in 1Q. Nominal GDP growth is 
likely to remain in double digits, 
significantly outpacing real GDP growth, 
thanks to improved terms of trade amid 
rising memory export prices. By 
expenditure, exports picked up in 3Q in 
both nominal and real terms, supported 
by stronger semiconductor shipments. 
Investment also appeared robust, as 
reflected in the equipment investment 
estimation index and machinery order 
index. Private consumption, however, 
appeared to weaken amid the sharp 
KOSPI correction since July following  
the introduction of leveraged ETF 
regulations. Our full -year GDP growth 
forecasts of 3.5% for 2026 and 2.4% for 
2027 remain on track.

Taiwan

The preliminary estimate for 3Q GDP is 
likely to show growth remaining strong 
at 12 –13% yoy , close to 12.9% in 2Q, 
albeit below the peak of 15.4% in 1Q. 
Exports maintained strong double -digit 
growth in 3Q (44.6% yoy  in nominal 
terms and 21.1% in real terms), supported 
by robust shipments of semiconductors, 
servers and other AI -related products. 
Machinery investment also sustained 
strong double-digit growth, underpinned 
by higher capacity utilisation in the ICT 
sector and a resulting increase in capital 
goods imports. Private consumption 
appeared to have strengthened further, 
supported by stock market gains, 
improving wage growth, and the 
associated wealth and income effects. 
Construction investment remained the 
weak s pot, weighed down by elevated 
inventories in the new-home market and 
the ongoing need for inventory digestion. 
We maintain our 2026 and 2027 GDP 
growth forecasts at 11.6% and 5.6%, 
respectively, with risks tilted to the 
upside.

Document 433: US MARKETS CALL_ S&P 500 Valuation Dives As Earnings Soar

Source PDF: all report pdf/US MARKETS CALL_ S&P 500 Valuation Dives As Earnings Soar.pdf
Report source: researchmail轉寄
Date: 2026-11-07
Pages: 12
Status: ok

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: US MARKETS CALL: S&P 500 Valuation Dives As Earnings Soar
Date:    5 hours ago
US MARKETS CALL: S&P 500 Valuation Dives As Earnings Soar

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
US MARKETS CALL: S&P 500
Valuation Dives As Earnings
Soar
By Ed Yardeni & Toby Hearst • 7 Nov 2026
View in browser

### Page 2

Photo by Baptiste Azais / Unsplash
The stock market dived last week. Investors priced in political backlash
against data centers after Democrats swept Congress. We anticipated
buying opportunities after the midterm elections, and Thursday’s selloff
delivered some. This remains an earnings-led bull market. Valuations are
falling while earnings are soaring. We still view the AI backlash as another

### Page 3

speed bump, not a roadblock. We are sticking with our 7,900 year-end
target for the S&P 500.
I. Performance
The Nasdaq entered correction territory on Thursday and closed the week
below its 200-day moving average (chart). It has done this before recently.
It rebounded sharply after its Spring 2025 "Liberation Day" plunge and
again after the Iran war started earlier this year, quickly resuming its
uptrend both times.
The equal-weight S&P 500 is holding up remarkably well. It remains near

its recent record high and above both its 50-day and 200-day moving
averages (chart). The damage has been concentrated in the AI trade, not
the broader market.

### Page 4

The Magnificent-7 are underperforming again. The MAGS ETF is up only
2.4% ytd, versus 7.8% for the S&P 500 (chart). The Impressive-493 also
took a hit this week. The XMAGS ETF includes Micron and the other
semiconductor stocks hit hardest in the selloff. It is still up 10.0% ytd.
Dow Theory remains bullish. The Dow Jones Industrial Average is holding
up well near its highs (chart). The Transports have lagged. They should
improve if oil prices continue to fall.

II. Earnings
Earnings season picked up this week. The blended Q3 S&P 500 EPS
growth estimate (i.e., actual results for companies that have reported,
estimates for those that haven’t yet) rose to 25.9% y/y from 23.8% a week
earlier, helped by AMD’s solid results. Q4’s estimate edged down to 29.7%

### Page 5

(chart). As we noted previously, Q2’s 50.4% y/y EPS growth rate was
boosted by mark-to-market gains at Alphabet and Amazon. Excluding
those, growth was closer to 25%, which is still very strong.
Forward earnings rose to a record $418.25. It is converging on the 2027
estimate of $426.28, which continues to climb (chart). Analysts’ 2028
consensus S&P 500 EPS estimate rose to $495.14; it’s been closing in on
$500—which is our target for 2030!  We are bullish, but not as bullish as

the analysts. The drop in the forward P/E this year suggests that investors
also believe analysts are too bullish on earnings.
Forward earnings for the S&P 500, S&P 400, and S&P 600 all rose to
record highs again last week (chart). Earnings strength remains broad.

### Page 6

S&P 500 forward revenues per share is up 15.9% y/y, while forward
earnings per share is up 39.0% y/y (chart).
III. Valuation
The S&P 500 forward P/E fell to 17.6 last week, below the 2025 Liberation
Day low (chart). P/E plunges typically accompany corrections and
recessions. The economy shows no sign of a recession anytime soon.
Investors instead fear irrational exuberance in earnings forecasts.

### Page 7

Since the bull market began on October 12, 2022, the S&P 500 has risen
106.2%. Forward earnings is up 75.5%, while the forward P/E is up 22.5%
(chart). The P/E and E both drove the rally until the forward P/E peaked in
October last year. Since then, E has done all the work. Investors are
finding it harder to predict the return on all the AI capital spending. They
won’t pay an excessive multiple given that uncertainty.
The S&P 500 Semiconductors industry accounts for much of the P/E drop.

The industry index's forward P/E has plunged to 13.1, well below the S&P
500’s (chart). Semis are now cheaper than they were during the 2022
bear market.

### Page 8

IV. Fixed Income
The futures market expects about two rate hikes over the next 12 months.
That explains why the 2-year Treasury yield remains well above the 3.88%
federal funds rate, even after easing to 4.46% following the midterms
(chart).
The 10-year Treasury yield continues to fall. It dropped to 4.84% and is
testing its uptrend line from last year’s low (chart). We’ll see if it bounces.
Either way, it is back in the 4.00%-5.00% “old normal” range where we feel

comfortable.

### Page 9

The 10-year TIPS yield fell to 2.62%. The yield spread between the 10-
year Treasury and TIPS, a widely used proxy for expected inflation, eased
to 2.22% (chart). That proxy tends to move with oil prices. The recent drop
in crude showed up here.
V. Commodities
The price of Brent crude oil fell to $98.32 a barrel in recent days (chart).
No news of the re-escalation of the war in the Middle East has emerged
since the midterms. Hopes for a US-Iran deal are reflected in the oil

market. We still see a risk of re-escalation.

### Page 10

Nearby gasoline futures dropped to $2.78 a gallon last week (chart). If
sustained, that should start to show up as lower inflation in a couple of
months. Renewed fighting in the Middle East would reverse that.
Steel prices show no sign of a slowdown in data center construction. US
Midwest hot-rolled coil rose to $1,357 a ton, the highest since 2022
(chart).

### Page 11

Agricultural prices have eased. The S&P GSCI Agriculture Spot index fell
to 376.77 last week (chart). Fears of a food price spike should subside.

Document 522: ブラジルの「トリプル安」は一過性の混乱にとどまるか ～ルラ氏の逆転勝利で財政懸念が再燃、硬直的な歳出構造が信認回復の足かせに～

Source PDF: all report pdf/ブラジルの「トリプル安」は一過性の混乱にとどまるか ～ルラ氏の逆転勝利で財政懸念が再燃、硬直的な歳出構造が信認回復の足かせに～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-10-27
Pages: 5
Status: ok

### Page 1

World Trends

2026 年10 月 27 日

1/5 
ブラジルの「トリプル安」は一過性の混乱にとどまるか 
～ルラ氏の逆転勝利で財政懸念が再燃、硬直的な歳出構造が信認回復の足かせに～ 
 経済調査部 主席エコノミスト  西濵 徹 
（℡：050-5474-7495）

（要旨） 
○ ブラジルでは、10 月 25 日の大統領選挙の決選投票において現職のルラ大統領が逆転勝利を
収めたことを受けて、 翌26 日の金融市場では株式、 通貨レアル、 国債が同時に売られる 「ト
リプル安」に直面した。背景には、右派フラビオ氏の勝利による財政健全化や金利低下への
期待を過度に織り込んだ取引が巻き戻されたことに加え、ルラ政権下で財政健全化の取り組
みが遅れるとの懸念がある。 
○ 同国では、政府債務の大きさや義務的経費の割合の高さが財政運営の制約となるなか、レア
ル安によるインフレ圧力の高まりが中銀の追加利下げ余地を狭めるとともに、金利上昇が景
気や財政を一段と圧迫するリスクが懸念される。一方、十分な外貨準備など対外ショックに
対する一定の耐性を有するほか、当局も市場安定化策を講じており、過度な相場変動が落ち
着く余地はある。 
○ ただし、市場の信認回復には具体的な財政健全化への取り組みが不可欠である。今後は、次
期政権の経済チームの人事や2027 年度予算の内容、 議会との合意形成を通じて、 財政健全化
に向けた道筋を示せるかが重要となる。

### Page 2

2/5 
【ルラ氏の逆転勝利を受けて、金融市場においてブラジル資産を売却する動きが広がる】 
ブラジルでは、10 月 25 日に実施された大統領選挙の決選投票において、左派ＰＴ（労働者党）
から出馬した現職のルラ大統領が、 右派
ＰＬ（自由党）のフラビオ・ボルソナロ
上院議員を破り、再選を決めた（注１）。
ブラジル高等選挙裁判所 （ＴＳＥ） が公
表した最終結果によれば、 ルラ氏の得票
率は 51.79％、フラビオ氏は 48.21％と
なった。これを受けて、翌 26 日の金融
市場では、株式、通貨レアル、国債のす
べてに売り圧力が強まった（図１）。 株式
市場では、 主要株価指数（ボベスパ指数）
が前営業日比で一時▲17％以上下落し、
取引を一時停止するサーキットブレーカーが発動された。 為替市場においてもレアルが大きく下落
するとともに、 債券市場では国債利回りや金利先物市場での金利上昇が進むなど、 「トリプル安」 に
直面した。

【右派政権への交代を過度に織り込んだ取引の巻き戻しが、相場変動を増幅させた】 
市場が大幅に変動した背景には、決選投票の前に積み上がった政権交代への期待が剥落したこと
がある。10 月４日の第１回投票では、事前予想に反してフラビオ氏が首位となった（注２） 。これ
を受けて、その後の市場では、同氏の勝利によって財政健全化が進み、将来的な金利低下につなが
るとの期待が強まる動きがみられた （注３）。 決選投票直前には、 ボベスパ指数は第１回投票前に比
べて約７％、レアルも対ドルで約５％上昇していた。 
投資家の間では、フラビオ氏の勝利を受けた財政規律の強化や経済改革が進む可能性を織り込み、
株式やレアルを買うとともに、金利低下を見込む取引を進めていた。しかし、ルラ氏の逆転勝利に
よってこうした前提が崩れ、複数の市場で同時に持ち高を調整する動きが強まったと考えられる。
よって、現職の続投という結果にもかかわらず、市場が政権交代を強く見込んでいたことで、大幅
な価格調整に繋がった。今回の下落幅を考える際には、ルラ政権の政策に対する評価に加え、選挙
前の取引が特定の結果に偏っていた可能性を考慮する必要がある。このため、市場では右派政権に
よる政策転換への期待が過度に先行していたと考えられる。

注１ 10 月26 日付レポート「ブラジル大統領選、ルラ氏が逆転勝利も政権運営には難路」 
  （https://www.dlri.co.jp/report/macro/666866.html） 
注２ 10 月５日付レポート「ブラジル大統領選は決選投票へ、フラビオ氏が首位」 
  （https://www.dlri.co.jp/report/macro/666284.html） 
注３ 10 月６日付レポート「金融市場はブラジルの右派回帰を好感、株価・レアルともに上昇」 
  （https://www.dlri.co.jp/report/macro/666333.html） 
図 1 主要株価指数とレアル相場(対ドル)の推移

### Page 3

3/5

【市場が抱く懸念には、公的財務の大きさに加え、 硬直的な歳出増による財政運営の難しさがある】 
市場がルラ氏の再選に厳しい反応を示した背景には、社会給付などの拡充が続くことにより、財
政健全化の取り組みが 遅れるとの懸念
がある。2026 年８月末時点における一般
政府の債務残高 （グロス値）はＧＤＰ比
82.9％に達している。さらに、ここ数年
の同国では、 インフレによる高金利が常
態化するなか、利払い負担の大きさが財
政運営を難しくしている。さらに、 近年
は司法判断に基づく支払債務 （プレカト
リオス）の増大も歳出を圧迫しており、
財政運営の硬直性を一段と強める要因
となっている。 
こうした状況では、 新たな恒久歳出に見合う財源を確保できるかが重要になる。 財政の持続可能
性に対する懸念が強まれば、投資家は国債の保有により高い利回りを求める。この結果、政府の新
規調達や借換えに伴う負担が増え、財政健全化を一段と困難にする可能性がある。株式、通貨、債
券が同時に売られたことは、 将来の政策運営に対する不確実性が、 ブラジル資産全体に求められる
リスクの上乗せ幅を拡大させたと解釈できる。 
財政運営の硬直性は、2027 年度予算案において、 基礎的歳出に占める義務的経費の割合が91.7％
に上ることにも現れている。このため、 裁量的な歳出削減による財政健全化の実現のハードルは極
めて高いと考えられる。今後は、政府が掲げる財政目標に加え、前述したプレカトリオスなど例外
措置を含めた実際の歳出、恒久財源の確保、債務残高の見通しを確認することが重要になる。ルラ
政権が主導する社会政策の効果を見極めつつ、給付の対象や制度設計、税制優遇、支出の優先順位
を見直すことができるかが、財政運営への信認を左右することが予想される。

【レアル安が利下げ余地を狭める一方、金利上昇が景気と財政を圧迫する懸念も残る】 
今回の混乱が、 金融政策の先行きに影響を与える可能性にも注意が必要である。 中銀は９月の定
例会合（COPOM）において、政策金利である Selic を 13.75％に引き下げた（注４）。一方で、会合
後に公表された声明においては、 持続的な通貨下落やインフレ期待の不安定化、 消費刺激策などを
物価の上振れリスクに挙げるとともに、 財政政策が金融政策や資産価格に及ぼす影響を注視する姿
勢を示した。

注４ ９月17 日付レポート「ブラジル中銀は５会合連続の利下げも、緩和継続には慎重姿勢」 
  （https://www.dlri.co.jp/report/macro/665843.html）

(%)(兆レアル) GDP比（右）

### Page 4

4/5 
足元では、イラン情勢の悪化を受けた原油高などを背景に、鈍化基調が続いてきたインフレ率が
再び加速に転じる兆しがみられる（図
３） 。 こうしたなか、レアル安の動きが長
期化すれば、 輸入品や輸入原材料の価格
上昇を通じて、 物価への押し上げ圧力が
強まることが予想される。 さらに、 企業
や家計 部門における インフレ予想が上
方修正されれば、 中銀が追加利下げを進
める余地は狭まる。次回の COPOM は 11
月３～４日に予定されており、 選挙後の
為替変動や期待インフレの動きをどう
評価するかが注目される。 中銀として
は、レアル安の継続性のほか、 物価への波及、 国内需要の減速などを総合的に判断する必要がある。 
実体経済への影響としては、 企業と家計の資金調達環境が厳しくなることが懸念される。 企業の
借入費用や債務の借換え負担が増えれば、 利益や設備投資に下押し圧力がかかる。家計向け融資の
金利上昇は、 住宅や耐久消費財の需要を抑える要因となる。こうした金融環境の悪化が長期化すれ
ば、 景気減速による税収の伸び悩みと利払い負担の増加が重なり、 財政運営をさらに難しくするこ
とも考えられる。 財政運営に対する懸念が金利を押し上げるとともに、 その金利上昇が景気と財政
を圧迫するという循環を抑えられるかが、今後の重要な課題となる。

【当局の市場安定化策と対外面での「緩衝材」はあるが、財政運営への信認回復が不可欠】 
市場の混乱を受けて、 当局は国債市場の安定化に向けた対応を進めている。26 日には固定利付国
債や物価連動国債の買い戻しを実施するとともに、27 日に予定していた物価連動国債と29 日の固
定利付国債の入札を中止する一方、27 日の政策金利連動国債の入札は維持した。これらの措置は、
売り注文が集中した市場において買い手を確保する一方、 急激な価格変動を抑えることに役立つと
考えられる。ただし、その効果を持続させるには、中期的な財政改善策を示すとともに、投資家が
国債を保有しやすい環境を整えることが必要になる。

### Page 5

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
5/5 
一方で、対外的な観点でみればブラジルには「緩衝材」が存在している。９月末時点における外
貨準備高は3,054 億ドルに上る。 この水
準は、ＩＭＦ（国際通貨基金）が国際金
融市場の動揺への耐性の有無の基準と
して示すＡＲＡ（適正水準評価）に照ら
して 「適正 （100～150％） 」 の域内にとど
まる（図４） 。そのうえ、９月までの 12
ヵ月累計の経常赤字は600 億ドルである
のに対し、対内直接投資の純流入は 883
億ドルとなっている。 これらの数字は選
挙前までの実績である ことに注意が必
要であるものの、 ブラジルが市場の急変
に対応するうえで一定の基盤を持つことを示唆している。ＩＭＦが７月に公表したⅣ条協議 （対ブ
ラジル審査） レポートにおいても、 変動相場制と十分な外貨準備が外部ショックを吸収する手段に
なると評価している。先行きについては、こうした基盤を踏まえつつ、財政不安と高金利が長期化
するリスクを見極める必要がある。

【先行きは次期政権における経済チームの人事、2027 年予算、議会との合意形成に要注意】 
今後注目すべき点は、 次期政権の経済チームの人選と、 大統領が財政改善策をどの程度政治的に
支えるかである。財務相などの人事は、政策運営の方向を示す材料となるとともに、その後に示さ
れる具体的な歳出・歳入措置も重要になる。そのうえで、2027 年予算案の修正、 新たな給付や支援
策の財源、義務的経費や税制優遇の見直しなどが、信認回復の判断材料となり得る。 
議会との関係も政策の実行可能性を左右することは避けられない。 次期下院においては、フラビ
オ氏が所属するＰＬが 121 議席と最大勢力となり、ＰＴを中心とする政党連合が 88 議席を獲得す
る見通しである。 ルラ政権としては、 予算や制度改正を進めるために他党との合意形成を図る必要
がある。右派勢力の影響力は、急激な歳出拡大を抑える要因となり得る。一方で、予算配分や税制
を巡る交渉が難航すれば、 財政健全化策の成立も遅れる可能性がある。 議会構成だけで財政規律の
改善を判断せず、 具体的な政策についてどのような合意が成立するかを確認することが重要である。 
現時点においては、 選挙前に積み上がった取引の巻き戻しが一巡すれば、 急激な値動きが落ち着
く余地はあると見込まれる。 しかし、 選挙前の相場を支えていた財政改善や金利低下への期待を取
り戻すには、政策面での裏付けが不可欠である。ルラ政権にとっては、有権者が期待する生活改善
の要求に応える一方、歳出の優先順位と財源を明確にしつつ、 政府債務の拡大を抑える道筋を示す
ことが求められる。 
以 上

景気は内需主因で停滞
◆消費・投資はコロナ禍以来の低い伸び
中国景気は内需の不振で足踏み 。小売売上高
は、財消費の低迷を主因に前年並みの水準で推移。
耐久消費財の買い替え補助金の効果がはく落して
いるほか、中東危機によるガソリン価格の高騰などを
背景に消費者マインドが一段と悪化 。固定資産投
資はインフラ投資などで底打ちの兆しがみられるもの
の、昨年以降大幅な減少が継続。
一方、外需は高い伸びを維持 。世界的なAIブー
ムを背景にIT関連財の輸出が引き続き好調である
ほか、EV需要の高まりを受けて自動車輸出台数も
3カ月連続で過去最高水準。もっとも、原油や半導
体などの価格上昇の影響も大きく 、実質ベースでは、
内需の減速を補うには至らず。
◆成長率目標の達成は困難
先行きの景気も、内需を中心に停滞が続く見通し。
ハイテク製造業の投資拡大や電力 ・通信網などの
「６つのネットワーク 」推進によるインフラ投資の持ち
直しが見込まれるものの 、不動産不況や過剰生産

能力といった構造的な下押し要因が残存し 、投資
は総じて低調に推移する見込み。悲観的な消費者
マインドが続くなか、消費も低迷が続く公算大。
一方、政府は景気刺激策に慎重 。成長率よりも
経済の質を重視し 、不動産市場や地方政府債務
などのリスク解消に注力する姿勢を明確にしており 、
2026年の成長率目標は達成困難な状況。
経済政策スタンス（鐘才文コラム）
（資料）人民網などを基に日本総研作成
（注）中央財経委員会が「鐘才文」名義で掲載した文章。
現状認識
・AI、ロボット、ハイテク製造業など新たな成長ドラ
イバーが台頭
・成長モデルの転換期にあり、成長率や月次指標
よりも経済運営の質を重視
・供給は強い一方、需要が弱い状況は残存
経済運営の
方向性
・AIや先端製造業を軸とした産業高度化を推進
・大規模な景気刺激策には依存しない
・不動産市場や地方政府債務などのリスク解消策
に注力（短期の成長率にはマイナスとなるも、長
期かつ持続的な経済発展にはプラス）
下半期の
経済見通し
・第15次５カ年計画などで確定した重点プロジェ

クトを執行すれば、経済は安定的に推移

消費は総じて低調
◆サービス消費は堅調も、財消費が低迷
８月の小売売上高は前年比＋ 0.3％と、ほぼゼ
ロ成長。財消費の低迷が主因であり、なかでも自動
車が大幅減。買い替え補助政策で喚起された需要
の反動減に加え、原油高でガソリン車需要が減退。
宝飾品も 、国際的な金価格の上昇と節約志向の
高まりが相まって、販売数量が大幅減。
先行きの消費も、総じて低調に推移する見通し。
消費者マインドは中東危機を契機に悪化基調 。６
月に一時持ち直したものの 、ガソリン価格の再上昇
を受けて再び低下 。政府は利子補給制度の対象
拡大などで内需を喚起しているものの 、大規模な消
費刺激策は打ち出さない姿勢。一方、サービス消費
は今後の消費拡大の重点分野に位置づけられてお
り、堅調に推移する見込み。
◆住宅市場は長期停滞から脱却できず
住宅市場は引き続き低迷。大都市を中心に住宅
購入要件の緩和などが実施されているものの 、購入
層の減少といった構造的な要因が住宅販売の重石

に。新築住宅価格に加え 、市場の実勢を反映しや
すい中古住宅価格も引き続き下落。在庫の回転期
間も長期化している模様。住宅市場の健全化に向
け、予約販売から完成後販売への移行や住宅ロー
ン返済期間の延長などが打ち出されたものの 、効果
が短期間で現れるとは見込めず 、住宅市場の本格
回復にはなお数年を要する見通し。
住宅販売に関する新たな政策
